   references carry it), AYRGAGR = form II; no-motif proteins form I only if >55% id / >=90% cov to Cordero refs.
   285 form-I proteins in 246 reps (was 1,901 / 1,170). Group 2a now its own column; two orphan `_sub` reps'
   proteins excluded. New table: `lifeonair/results/reads/guild_genome_equivalents_v2.tsv` (id_floor 60 is primary).

   - bulk medians GE: 1l 1.08, 1h 0.14, 2a 0.03, form-I CoxL 0.105, other NiFe (no 2a) 0.08, other CoxL 2.29
   - root vs bulk (106 visits / 47 sites): 1l -2.27; 1h -0.33; 2a -0.34; form I -0.36; controls -0.29;
     excess beyond controls: 1l -1.83 (p_site<1e-4), 1h -0.01, form I -0.05
   - surface vs bulk (68 visits / 36 sites): 1l -1.05, 1h -0.63, 2a -0.37, form I -0.51, controls -0.16;
     excess 1l -0.83, 1h -0.42, form I -0.24 (p 0.008)
3. Biomass/DNA yield (`lifeonair/results/inputs/sample_ledger_dna.tsv`, extract concentration; available for
   267 usable samples, trips 1-3 and few of trip 5, none of trip 4): root-adjacent extracts ~9x bulk (log2 +3.2);
   prokaryotic SCG reads per million are similar across positions (plant DNA does not explain it). DNA-scaled
   per-gram proxy, root vs bulk: 1l +0.4 (n.s.), 1h +1.5, 2a +2.3, form I +2.5.
   Exploratory mixed models (log2 GE ~ log2 DNA + position + trip + (1|site)): slope for 1l -0.32 (SE 0.03),
   within bulk only -0.23 (SE 0.05); all other markers -0.02 to -0.05. Across bulk sites, log DNA rises with
   MAP (rho 0.41, p 0.01) and site-median 1l falls with log DNA (rho -0.45, p 0.005).
4. The PI decided: H1 is reframed as relative exclusion (1l carriers do not take part in the larger root-zone
   community) rather than loss of capacity; H2 is narrowed (the root contrast is 1l-specific; CO capacity is no
   longer claimed to be position-independent). The PI asked for ADJACENT OR ALTERNATIVE HYPOTHESES that these
   data can test and that would be of ISME Journal quality.

## Candidate hypothesis from the authors (evaluate critically)

"Biomass-decoupled scavengers": group 1l carriers' per-gram abundance scales sub-proportionally with total
microbial biomass (1l GE ~ biomass^-0.3, other markers ~ biomass^0), so their share falls wherever productivity
is higher: beside roots, at wetter sites, at greener sites. If true, a single mechanism (an atmospheric-energy-
limited population that does not track organic-carbon-driven growth) would unify the root contrast, the aridity
gradient and the NDVI association, and the aridity association would be partly a biomass-dilution effect rather
than selection by aridity. Weigh measurement error in DNA yield (regression dilution, kit/trip effects,
relic DNA), circularity (GE denominators), and what would falsify it.

Use when asked to draft, revise, shorten, reorganize, polish, or review scientific prose; improve argument flow; remove markers of AI-written text; or make methods reproducible and results evidence-led. # Write and edit scientific papers

Use this skill as both a scientific writer and a structural editor. Preserve the
science while making the paper's reasoning explicit, proportionate to the
evidence, and easy to follow.

Robert's primary editing criterion is the logical flow of the argument. First
decide which claims, premises, and intermediate steps belong and how they support
the conclusion. Paragraph shape, transitions, and sentence polish come afterward.
A smoother passage is not an improvement if it retains an irrelevant detour.

Before drafting or editing, read
`references/robert-scientific-style.md` completely. It is a critical adaptation
of the house style, not a list of mechanical substitutions. In particular, apply
its rules for ordinary prose paragraphs, connected reasoning, and corrections
without revision history. The headings and checklists in this skill organize
agent instructions; they are not a template for manuscript formatting.

## Scope and related skills

This skill owns manuscript prose, argument structure, and scientific writing style.
Use `grounded-research` for reference/identifier audits or requested project setup;
its scaffolder exports this skill's style guide. A prose edit does not require a
new project layout or a full audit of unchanged identifiers. Use `write-paper-review`
when the requested deliverable is a referee report, and `new-paper` for recording
and announcing an accepted or published paper.

## Establish the source of truth

1. Identify the audience, venue, length limit, manuscript role, and requested
   scope. Inspect venue instructions when they are available.
2. Locate the authoritative data, tables, figures, result files, citations, and
   terminology. Treat generated values and manuscript macros as fixed unless the
   user asks to recompute them.
3. Separate established facts, results shown by the current study, plausible
   interpretations, and speculation.
4. Never invent a result, citation, method detail, data version, or novelty
   claim. Use an explicit placeholder only in a working draft. If a final claim
   requires unavailable evidence or a scientific choice, ask the user.

## Learning and applying Robert's style

Apply the revision-derived explanatory guidance in the shared style reference.
For substantial structural rewrites or further style calibration, consult
[the historical revision evidence](references/revision-evidence.md). Prefer
before/after edits to coauthor prose over surface imitation of finished papers.
Preserve necessary premises, define operations, and make the reason for the next
step explicit. Historical examples guide structure; current instructions govern
formatting and wording. Do not claim a stylistic improvement from metadata checks
alone.

## Build the argument before polishing sentences

For the passage being drafted or revised, identify its role in the paper's
contribution. Scale this planning to the request: a local edit needs the surrounding
paragraphs, not a new outline of the entire paper. For each relevant section or
subsection, identify:

- the question or claim it advances;
- the evidence or method that supports it;
- why that evidence changes what the reader should believe; and
- why the next paragraph or section follows.

Remove material that does not support this chain, or move necessary technical
detail to the Methods or Supplement. Do not move details whose absence would
prevent reproduction.

Before polishing, outline the argument independently of the existing paragraph
boundaries: the question, necessary premises or observations, intermediate steps,
and supported conclusion. Test whether each piece of the draft contributes to
that argument. Remove a detour when its removal leaves the reasoning intact;
do not manufacture a bridge to retain it merely because it is accurate and related
to the topic. Preserve scientific qualifications and information required for
reproduction. Add a missing premise only when the sources support it; otherwise
flag the gap. Then arrange paragraphs around the resulting progression and check
their internal dependencies. These planning notes are not manuscript headings.
An explicit argument need not be longer: explain only the steps the intended
reader needs to follow this particular inference.

## Use section-specific structures

### Introduction

Build the Introduction as a problem-driven argument:

1. State the problem from general to specific. Define it precisely for the
   paper type. For a computational problem, identify the inputs, outputs, and
   constraints governing how the outputs relate to the inputs. Do not force
   this input--output formulation onto resource, observational, or other papers
   for which it does not fit.
2. Explain how the problem has been addressed. Organize the state of the art
   around methods or approaches, not names. Explain how each approach works at
   the level needed for the argument, which part of the problem it solves,
   where it fails, and how later approaches build on it. Continue until the
   limitations of the current state of the art are explicit.
3. State how the new work addresses those limitations. Introduce the work
   directly, then map each established limitation to the corresponding
   component or advance. Do not claim capabilities unrelated to the gap just
   established.
4. State how the work is evaluated and what scientific or practical impact
   that evaluation can support. Keep the impact claims within the scope of the
   evidence.
5. State where the method, resource, software, and data are available, as
   applicable.

Remove prior work that does not support this argument or move it to the point
where its role becomes clear. Do not turn the Introduction into an exhaustive
review, and state novelty only when the literature search supports it.

### Methods

Write the Methods so an informed reader can reproduce every reported result
without guessing. For each method, specify, as applicable, its purpose and
output; data or other inputs, sources, versions, access dates, sampling, and
inclusion or exclusion criteria; preprocessing and curation; protocol,
algorithm, or formal definition; software, instruments, databases, and models
with exact versions; parameters, prompts, seeds, configurations, and stopping
criteria; manual and automated steps with their decision rules; provenance
from inputs to outputs; and quality control, baselines, evaluation design,
metrics, and statistical analyses. State missing details of the reported
experiment when needed for reproducibility. Describe the method actually used;
keep the history of correcting its description out of the manuscript. Keep the essential design in
the main Methods; put commands, checksums, long parameter listings, and
diagnostics in the Supplement only when the main text and Supplement together
remain sufficient for reproduction.

Define every formal object unambiguously at first use. If several objects could
share a generic label such as `model`, give each a distinct name and state the
relationships or mappings among them. Do not use an unqualified generic term
when it could refer to more than one object.

### Results

Use the following reasoning sequence across a Results passage. Express it in
connected prose; the labels below are planning prompts, not visible headings or
a requirement for five sentences in every paragraph:

1. **Hypothesis or question:** state what the analysis tests or aims to show.
2. **How:** describe the comparison, experiment, or analysis briefly.
3. **Finding:** point to the relevant figure or table and state the pattern.
4. **Meaning:** state what the pattern supports, in positive terms (see
   "State what results show" below).
5. **Bridge:** when appropriate, identify the next uncertainty and use it to
   motivate the next hypothesis.

Do not narrate every value already visible in a table or figure. Report numbers
in prose only when the reader needs them to follow the inference. Distinguish
an accuracy test from an illustration, sensitivity analysis, negative control,
or mechanistic example.

### Discussion

Return to the paper's main claim, relate it to the closest prior work, delimit
what the evaluation establishes, state material limitations, and identify
specific next tests. Do not repeat the Results section as a list of values.

## Edit in five passes

1. **Structure:** repair the claim–evidence sequence and paragraph order.
2. **Evidence:** verify that claims match figures, tables, citations, and the
   evaluation design; weaken claims that exceed their controls.
3. **Reproducibility:** ensure that Methods and Supplement jointly specify the
   data, versions, protocol, metrics, exclusions, and code availability.
4. **Paragraphs:** check that each sentence develops the paragraph's main point
   and follows from information already introduced. Read adjacent paragraphs
   together: the next must take up a result, unresolved question, or dependency
   established by the previous one. Repair the reasoning before adding a transition
   word. Use a closing interpretation only when it advances the argument.
5. **Prose:** remove redundancy, nominalizations, vague intensifiers, inflated
   verbs, unnecessary hedges, and formulaic transitions.

Preserve labels, citations, macros, terminology, and numerical values during a
prose edit. Flag any edit that changes a scientific endpoint, comparison,
interpretation, or claim strength.

When Robert corrects a mistake, replace the mistaken account with the supported
account and update affected explanations within scope. The correction is an editing
instruction, not a new historical fact to narrate. Report any material change to
Robert separately from the manuscript. See the style guide's correction examples.

## State what results show

State positively what data, results, and analyses show. Never write a sentence
whose job is to tell the reader what the data or a result does *not* show,
mean, measure, or imply. Sentences of this kind pre-empt a misreading that the
writer has imagined for the reader; the house style does not make assumptions
about what the reader is thinking, so such sentences are removed rather than
softened.

Forbidden forms, in any section, in the main text, the supplement, and a data
paper alike:

- contrastive negation: "These shares describe the marker-gene profile, not
  cell counts"; "This is a selection outcome rather than a rate"
- negated inference: "does not show", "does not mean", "does not imply",
  "cannot be read as", "should not be interpreted as", "is not evidence of"
- disclaimers of scope attached to a result: "This test does not establish
  causation"; "These values are not comparable across trips"

Replace each with the positive statement that carries the same information:

- "These shares describe the relative abundance of the 16S marker gene."
- "Communities in this treatment were selected on nitrate."
- "The comparison is within trip; each trip was sequenced on one run."

If the positive statement is already in the text, delete the negated sentence.
If the positive statement conveys nothing the reader needs, delete the sentence
without replacement. A limitation belongs in the Discussion, phrased as what the
design does establish and what a named further experiment would add.

Apply this rule during the Evidence and Prose passes, and check for it
explicitly before delivering any draft or revision.

## Remove markers of AI-written prose

Look for generic scene-setting, repeated transition words, uniform paragraph
shapes, symmetrical lists without scientific need, restated captions,
unsupported claims of novelty or robustness, and sentences that sound polished
but do not advance the argument. Replace them with domain-specific subjects,
concrete actions, calibrated claims, and transitions that express the actual
logical relation. Vary sentence length naturally; do not introduce quirks or
informality merely to appear human.

## Check the actual output before delivery

Read the revised passage, not only the diff. Check these three recurring failures:

- Remove paragraph-opening bold labels and run-in headings, including Markdown
  `**Label.**`, LaTeX `\paragraph{...}` / `\paragraph*{...}`, and label-like
  `\textbf{...}`. Use ordinary prose paragraphs. Keep genuine section/subsection
  headings; use run-in headings only when Robert explicitly requests them or the
  venue requires them. Preserve mathematical bold notation.
- State each paragraph's main point and its relationship to its neighbour. If the
  relationship is only "another fact about the topic", rebuild the progression.
  Check sentence dependencies within paragraphs as well as paragraph order.
- Inspect "previously", "earlier version", "now", "corrected", and similar wording
  in context. Remove references whose only source is an editing exchange or a
  superseded draft. Preserve supported prior-work comparisons, reported version
  comparisons, and relevant scientific chronology.

For a local edit, also read the preceding and following paragraph. Do not claim
these checks passed for material outside the inspected scope.

## Deliver the revision

Lead with the revised text or completed artifact. Briefly report structural or
scientific changes that affect how the argument is read in a separate author-facing
note, never inserted into the manuscript. List unresolved
evidence gaps or decisions only when they require the user's input. Do not
explain every line edit unless requested.

name: grounded-research
description: Verify research references, DOIs, ontology IRIs/CURIEs, accessions, and URLs against their sources, or scaffold a research project when explicitly requested. Use for bibliography and identifier audits. Manuscript drafting, editing, and writing style belong to write-edit-scientific-paper.
---

# Grounded research

Own reference and identifier verification and explicitly requested project setup.
Use [write-edit-scientific-paper](../write-edit-scientific-paper/SKILL.md) for prose,
argument structure, and scientific writing style. Use `write-paper-review` for a
referee report. A prose-editing request alone does not require scaffolding or a
full identifier audit.

## Verify the requested material

Keep source artifacts, metadata, and the claims they support together. Retrieve
bibliographic metadata from the DOI or publisher, and read the cited material to
check support for the specific claim. An identifier resolving successfully checks
identity; it does not establish that the source supports a manuscript's claim.

Run the relevant checkers on the requested files or identifiers. Distinguish an
invalid identifier from a network failure or unavailable source. Report unresolved
checks and retain the original evidence; do not invent a replacement or silently
remove a scientific claim. The researcher remains responsible for the work.

When drafting introduces or changes a citation or identifier, verify that item.
Preserve already verified items during a prose-only edit. A complete bibliography
or identifier audit is a separate requested task or a project's explicit gate.

## Set up a project only when requested

First inspect any existing project layout and its instructions. Run
`scripts/setup_project.py <dir>` only for a requested new scaffold, in an empty
or new directory. It creates `paper/`, `code/`, and `data/`; `--git` initializes
local paper and code repositories. It prints a remote-creation suggestion without
creating or pushing a remote repository.

The generated `paper/WRITINGSTYLE.md` is a standalone snapshot of the canonical
[scientific style guide](../write-edit-scientific-paper/references/robert-scientific-style.md).
The compatibility file `WRITINGSTYLE.md` in this skill links to that same guide.
Existing manuscript style files are not overwritten during ordinary edits.

## Project scaffolder (`scripts/setup_project.py`)

Offline-safe; creates the `paper/` + `code/` + `data/` layout, copies the canonical
scientific style guide into `paper/WRITINGSTYLE.md`, and initializes the two repos
with `--git`. Run commands from this skill directory.

```
python3 scripts/setup_project.py my-project --title "My Project" --git
```

## Checker scripts (`scripts/`, stdlib-only Python 3)

Each exits non-zero if anything fails; each accepts explicit IDs or `--file`.

- **Full identifier audit:**
  ```
  python3 scripts/check_all.py paper.tex references.bib [--owl myonto.owl]
  ```
  Extracts and verifies DOIs, ontology IRIs/CURIEs, accessions, and URLs from
  the given files; prints a per-identifier report and an overall PASS/FAIL.

- **DOIs / references** (doi.org content negotiation = what doi2bib wraps):
  ```
  python3 scripts/check_dois.py references.bib      # verify each doi + title match
  python3 scripts/check_dois.py --doi 10.1126/science.adg7879   # fetch real BibTeX
  ```
  For a `.bib` it flags any DOI that does not resolve and any title that does not
  match doi.org (a sign of a wrong/fabricated entry).

- **Ontology IRIs / CURIEs** (EBI OLS4; optional local OWL and BioPortal):
  ```
  python3 scripts/check_iris.py GO:0008150 CHEBI:15377 http://purl.obolibrary.org/obo/UBERON_0002107
  python3 scripts/check_iris.py --file paper.tex --owl myonto.owl
  ```
  Flags terms that do not exist or are **obsolete**; prefers the defining
  ontology for the label. `--bioportal` cross-checks if `BIOPORTAL_APIKEY` is set.

- **Database accessions** (UniProt, Ensembl REST, NCBI E-utilities for RefSeq):
  ```
  python3 scripts/check_accessions.py P04637 ENSG00000141510 NM_000546
  python3 scripts/check_accessions.py --file paper.tex
  ```
  Auto-detects the type and confirms each accession resolves to a real entity
  (reports the canonical name); `--db` forces a backend.

- **URLs / links**:
  ```
  python3 scripts/check_urls.py --file paper.tex references.bib
  ```
  FAILs dead links (no response, 404/410/5xx). Treats 401/403/429 as a
  non-fatal WARN (bot protection, not a missing page) and cross-domain
  redirects as a WARN to verify manually.

### Notes / honesty

- A FAIL means "could not be verified against the authoritative source" — report
  it, investigate, and either ground it in a real source or drop it. Never paper
  over a FAIL by generating a plausible substitute.
- Network/timeout failures are reported distinctly from "invalid"; if a service
  is down (it happens), say so rather than declaring the identifier fake.
- Scripts are stdlib-only (`urllib`) and rate-limited; no external dependencies.
- AberOWL is the group's own resource; OLS4 is the reliable default here. If the
  AberOWL REST endpoint is needed, wire it into `check_iris.py` (the maintainer
  knows its API).

## Don't

- Don't ask the model to invent content, references, numbers, or identifiers.
- Don't trust any DOI/IRI/accession/URL the model produced without a tool check.
- Don't let the model write prose from nothing; prose comes from real artifacts.
- Don't answer a question (committee, reviewer, supervisor) via the LLM; the
  understanding must be the human's.
- Don't treat the tool as an author or as accountable. The human is both.

 succeeded in 0ms:
**Part 1 — Review**

The manuscript titled “Plant roots and long-term aridity set the atmospheric hydrogen-scavenging capacity of hyperarid Rub’ al-Khali soils” combines a 5,438-genome catalogue with read-based functional profiling of 550 metagenomes. It reports a pronounced root-associated reduction in group-1l hydrogenase abundance, contrasting distributions of hydrogen and carbon monoxide oxidation genes, and an association between group-1l abundance and long-term precipitation. Vegetation indices and predicted growth traits are used to investigate possible mechanisms. The strongest contribution is the replicated, depth-matched group-1l contrast. However, an inverted CoxL classification rule compromises the CO results, and the temporal analysis covers substantially less time than the manuscript implies.

I recommend **major revision**. The root-associated group-1l pattern appears recoverable, but the manuscript’s present three-hypothesis narrative is not supported. H2 requires reconstruction after correcting CoxL classification. H3 must distinguish an association with spatial precipitation from evidence about temporal responses or climate-change substitution. Several mechanistic statements exceed what these observational, relative-abundance data establish.

The audit reproduced the original read aggregation to its reported precision: maximum GE discrepancy was below \(5\times10^{-6}\). All 573 archived screening metadata records specified 100-nt truncation and a 25-aa minimum alignment; the archive and local database tables matched their recorded checksums. I also reproduced the principal group-1l contrast and spatial correlation. These checks establish numerical traceability, not biological validity of the labels. I did not rerun DIAMOND, tree inference, or the complete R workflows. No files were modified. The PDF integrity scan detected no reviewer-directed instructions; its manuscript flags concerned small figure text.

Major issues follow.

**Marker identity must be corrected before interpreting the ecology (R1-01, R1-02).** The CoxL classifier reverses the diagnostic motifs. `AYRGAGR` is associated with form II, whereas the characteristic form-I motif is `AYXCSFR`. This distinction is explicit in primary sequence analyses. [Martin-Cuadrado et al.](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC2786428/) The error is consequential: 1,627 of 1,901 reported form-I proteins were admitted solely by `AYRGAGR`, without passing the identity/coverage criterion.

I performed a diagnostic relabelling in memory, retaining the identity/coverage criterion and recognising the form-I motif, without changing alignments. This reduced the candidate form-I set to 285 proteins. Approximately 90.1% of reads originally assigned to form I across the screened libraries were reassigned. Bulk-soil median form-I GE changed from 0.616 to 0.105. The surface/bulk contrast changed from −0.020 to −0.502 log₂ units, with site-level Wilcoxon \(p=0.00106\). The root/bulk contrast became −0.344, \(p=0.0284\). These are diagnostic recalculations, not a substitute for curated reclassification, but they invalidate the current claim of position-independent CO capacity. The group-1l root contrast remained approximately −2.23, and its excess remained approximately −1.97 under this correction.

The hydrogenase control also needs revision. Group 2a includes experimentally demonstrated atmospheric H₂ oxidisers, yet all group-2a reads enter the supposedly non-high-affinity control. [Islam et al.](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7784904/) The dataset contains 140 group-2a proteins, and their reads account for 24.1% of the primary cohort’s other-[NiFe] control reads. Moreover, Grinter et al., cited for the introductory enzyme statement, characterise Huc, a group-2a enzyme. Restricting the focal comparison to groups 1h and 1l is legitimate; defining them as the complete atmospheric-H₂ guild and placing group 2a in a negative control is not.

**The temporal test is not a five-expedition, 31-month test (R1-03, R1-04, R1-11).** All expedition-5 antecedent precipitation values are missing because the climate record ends in 2024. The bulk-soil temporal analysis contains 71 visits at 27 sites between March 2023 and August 2024, approximately 17 months. Fourteen sites contribute only two visits. This exclusion must be explicit in the abstract, methods, results, and interpretation, or the precipitation record must be extended.

Comparing a 30-year spatial climate normal with preceding-year rainfall does not directly test the response to a sustained change in aridity. Nor does a nonsignificant temporal coefficient establish independence from rain. The manuscript also compares the temporal confidence interval with a spatial point estimate rather than estimating their difference. My joint site bootstrap retained a positive temporal-minus-spatial difference, approximately 1.05–6.31, so propagating spatial uncertainty alone does not eliminate that contrast. However, this still compares different environmental quantities and treats sites as independent despite only four shared expeditions. The defensible conclusion is a difference between the sampled spatial and short-term associations, subject to the design’s limitations.

Within-site centring does not make visit residuals independent. Their ordinary Spearman \(p\)-values remain inappropriate for repeated observations. Conversely, the trip-adjusted sample-level mixed model uses uncentred precipitation, mixing within-site and between-site information. These analyses should be replaced by an explicit within/between decomposition with the relevant replication structure.

**The hypotheses are exploratory, and the primary cohort is inconsistently defined (R1-05, R1-06).** The results README states that the original hypotheses were contradicted and replacement hypotheses were developed after reassessing the same data. Repeated statements that results occurred “as H1/H3 predicted” therefore misrepresent the inferential sequence. Report this as exploratory hypothesis development and distinguish any genuinely independent validation.

The 61-location analysis includes four samples from sites 62 and 64. The companion data descriptor identifies these as special-interest locations outside the repeated 1–60 transect; the site-alias table identifies a road location and a campground. Restricting to sites 1–60 gives 546 samples at 59 sites and 106 root/bulk visits at 47 sites. The group-1l contrast remains strong, approximately −2.275 log₂, but the manuscript needs a consistent sampling frame and a documented inclusion ledger.

**Relative depletion does not establish loss of organisms or selection against the enzyme (R1-07, R1-08, R1-09).** GE is a relative marker-copy measure, and the lineage analyses are also compositional. The available DNA records make biomass differences a concrete concern: after excluding conflicting/nonpositive concentration records, 48 paired site visits had a median root/bulk DNA-concentration ratio of approximately 9.78. This is not a valid absolute microbial-biomass correction, because extraction and plant DNA can contribute, but it demonstrates why lower copies per genome cannot be equated with fewer carriers per gram.

Matching depth removes one confounder. It does not isolate plant carbon from moisture, oxygen, soil properties, plant identity, or microsite selection. The statements that plants “select against” group 1l and that aridity “sets” its abundance require stronger evidence or associative wording. Likewise, attenuation after adjustment for composition PC1 does not demonstrate mediation through lineage composition. It is compatible with mediation, confounding, and adjustment for a variable partly constructed from the same carrier abundances.

**The mechanism analyses do not quantify the contribution of plant carbon (R1-10, R1-22).** A 250-m NDVI measurement and predicted maximal growth potential are imperfect proxies for carbon availability at a root microsite. A residual intercept of −1.59 does not estimate the fraction of the effect unexplained by carbon, particularly without measurement-error treatment or a mediation model. “Plant-carbon proxies explain a minor part” should be replaced by a narrower statement about the observed associations.

The genus analysis also needs stronger treatment of phylogeny, prevalence, and uncertainty. It treats 288 genus summaries as observations, but 123 have exactly zero median fold change and 31 genera have only one representative contributing to their traits. The response, growth predictor, and carriage predictors all depend on the recovered MAG catalogue, with incomplete genomes treated as non-carriers when a marker is absent. Phylum adjustment cannot establish an enzyme-specific effect beyond lineage ecology.

**Several statistical analyses do not respect the experimental structure (R1-12–R1-18).** The main paired H1 analysis appropriately resamples sites. This protection is not carried consistently into the NDVI, H2, H3, and mechanism analyses, where co-located samples share environmental values but several models omit a site-visit effect. The spatial bootstrap over six blocks is useful sensitivity evidence, but needs residual spatial diagnostics and sensitivity to block definition. Independent climate products support the spatial association; they do not validate the temporal rainfall exposure.

The PERMANOVA uses unrestricted permutations, although samples are nested within visits and repeatedly collected at sites. Its 29.4% site fraction and 2.0% expedition fraction also correspond to very different numbers of factor degrees of freedom. Their ratio is not evidence that lineages persisted or that ecological integration takes longer than 31 months.

The synchrony calculation pools positions within each visit despite strong position effects and uneven sampling. Changes in compartment representation can therefore generate synchrony. More generally, synchrony measures covariation, not persistence.

Even before correcting CoxL, its compartment LRT was significant, \(p=4.87\times10^{-6}\). A changing H₂/CO ratio demonstrates different proportional responses, not independent regulation or equivalence of CO abundance across positions. Finally, secondary tests require multiplicity control. For example, the highlighted surface-CoxL temporal \(p=0.00983\) becomes approximately 0.0738 under BH adjustment across just the nine trip-adjusted marker-by-position tests.

**Sequence provenance and measurement validity need further work (R1-19–R1-21, R1-23, R1-24).** The `_sub` annotation resolvers can retrieve parent-bin annotations without restricting them to retained contigs. Two representatives explicitly removed from the official hydrogenase guild still contribute three group-1l proteins to the read database. The growth workflow also processes 1,010 `_sub` representatives through this resolver. The catalogue census, trait analyses, and sequence database therefore need reconciliation at the retained-contig level.

Competitive matching within this targeted database does not establish sensitivity and specificity against broader homologous families. The marker and ribosomal reference sets also have different coverage and clustering procedures. Benchmark the short-read classifier and GE calibration, particularly across the taxonomic compositions being compared.

The assertion that the shared denominator cancels exactly is false for the implemented pseudocount-adjusted ratios of replicate medians. Without a pseudocount, the paired group-1l median is −2.529 rather than −2.231; no paired cell requires a pseudocount for that contrast. The qualitative pattern survives, but the headline magnitude and invariance argument require qualification.

Contamination screening is incomplete across batches, and the nine available PMA pairs are not analysed. These limitations matter particularly for claims about persistence and root-associated loss in low-biomass soil.

**The literature supports a narrower novelty claim and a less directional moisture mechanism (R1-25, R1-26).** Li et al. already combine desert shrub contrasts, carbon measurements/additions, trace-gas uptake, and life-history traits. López et al. directly establish predicted growth potential as a major correlate of rhizosphere enrichment. The contribution here should centre on the replicated group-resolved association in this transect, after classification correction. Li et al. also performed hydrogenase subgroup typing; their reported groups require explicit comparison rather than characterising their analysis simply as one undifferentiated hydrogen function.

Jordaan et al. report relative declines in group-1l genes/transcripts after hydration, but also strongly stimulated H₂ uptake and explicitly caution about relative expression. Imminger et al. report increased group-1l transcription during hydration. These studies do not support a general rule that wetter root-adjacent soil suppresses group-1l physiology. Stable DNA composition can coexist with rapid metabolic responses.

**Reproducibility remains incomplete despite the useful frozen outputs (R1-27, R1-28).** The archived counts and checksums are valuable. However, the advertised fetch route requires KAUST credentials, upstream typing is outside the supplied analysis-code directory, several inputs use private absolute paths, and P3 claims are absent from the repository’s claim-verification harness. The sensitivity code also does not rerun all H1–H3 analyses as the methods claim: it omits the temporal analyses and the primary H2 raw-read-ratio contrast.

Minor issues follow.

The operon screen establishes neighbourhood co-occurrence, not complete operons: it does not require the reported gene order or strand consistency (R1-29). Figures need larger text at final manuscript size, annotated phylogenetic support/reference groups, uncertainty intervals, and a temporal panel corresponding to the headline H3 claim (R1-30). The reported compartment “variance shares” omit covariance between the compartment and expedition fitted components, so they should not be presented as a standard partition of explained variance (R1-31).

The README and manuscript should agree on the current surface contrast and spatial bootstrap interval; distinguish median-effect intervals from tests on site means (R1-32). Finally, Jordaan’s study is Australian, not Gobi, and predicted maximal growth potential should consistently be distinguished from observed growth in these soils (R1-33).

# Round 1 literature digest (Gemini 3.8 Flash via agy, 8 search directions, 12 Oct 2026)

Candidates were proposed by Flash and every DOI was checked against Crossref (title match); 5 mismatched DOIs were corrected by hand and none of the retained DOIs is fabricated. **The one-line relevance notes are Flash summaries and are NOT verified**: check the local PDF/XML before relying on any claim. Local files are in `lifeonair/literature/<key>.pdf` or `<key>.fulltext.xml`; "no local copy" means paywalled (citation + DOI only).

Search prompts: `search/round1/prompts/`; raw outputs with Flash syntheses: `search/round1/out/`.

## H1 (19)

| key | DOI | stance | relevance (Flash, unverified) | local |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| delaporte_2024_rhizosphere-effect-promotes-persistence | 10.1016/j.soilbio.2024.109599 | challenges | Demonstrates that rhizosphere carbon inputs can promote rather than suppress the persistence of trace gas oxidizing activity in nutrient-poor soils, challenging the universality of root-adjacent capacity depletion. | no local copy |
| lopez_2023_growth-rate-dominant-factor | 10.1038/s41396-023-01453-6 | challenges | Finds that predicted maximal growth rate is the primary driver of rhizosphere enrichment across global soil MAGs, directly challenging our finding that growth rate explains only a minor part of root-adjacent shifts. | PDF |

| (already in bib/MANIFEST) | 10.1186/s40168-018-0605-2 | method | Develops the statistical frequency and prevalence algorithms implemented in decontam to systematically remove reagent and environmental contaminant reads from low-biomass metagenomes. | known |
| zhou_2022_metabolic-high-throughput-profiling | 10.1186/s40168-021-01213-8 | method | Implements standard HMM-based profiling and read mapping architectures to quantify microbial biogeochemical functional markers, including [NiFe]-hydrogenases and CoxL. | PDF |
| kaminski_2015_high-specificity-targeted-functional | 10.1371/journal.pcbi.1004557 | method | Demonstrates that profiling homologous protein families from short metagenomic reads requires competitive reduction against background protein databases to prevent false positive mapping. | PDF |
| gloor_2017_microbiome-datasets-compositional-this | 10.3389/fmicb.2017.02224 | method | Demonstrates that relative abundance data inherently produce false-positive negative correlations due to constant-sum constraints, necessitating single-copy normalisation or log-ratios. | PDF |
| xue_2021_diting-pipeline-infer-compare | 10.3389/fmicb.2021.698286 | method | Provides an automated pipeline for calculating the relative abundance of microbial biogeochemical pathways from metagenomic reads and contigs using curated marker genes. | PDF |
| giguere_2020_acidobacteria-active-abundant-members | 10.1038/s41396-020-00750-8 | supports | Demonstrates through mRNA transcript profiling and kinetic assays that DNA-based group 1h large subunit profiling reliably identifies functionally active atmospheric H2 scavengers across soil bacterial phyla. | PDF |
## Cross-direction themes raised by Flash (to be checked, not established)

1. **Compositional artefact for H1**: relative (per-genome) profiling can show oligotroph "loss" when rhizosphere biomass expands (Azarbad 2022); growth rate is reported as a dominant predictor of the rhizosphere effect (Lopez et al. 2023 ISME J), which bears on the claim that growth rate explains only a minor part of the 1l loss.
2. **Group 1l physiology**: Imminger 2024 (Nat Commun) reportedly finds desert 1l expression responsive to hydration; Kropp 2026 (mSystems) catabolite repression of high-affinity hydrogenase by glycerol; Baril & Constant 2023 labile-carbon repression; de la Porte 2024 rhizosphere effect promoting gas-oxidation persistence (contrary direction).
3. **Space-for-time**: failure of substitution is predicted by theory when temporal variance is small relative to the spatial gradient (Blois 2013, Damgaard 2019, Perret 2024, Lovell 2023, Knapp 2026 aridity vs drought); legacy effects (Hawkes 2017, Canarini 2021). Risk that "space-for-time fails" is framed as more novel/strong than a 31-month design permits.
4. **H2 novelty**: broader taxonomic spread/redundancy of form-I CoxL than of high-affinity hydrogenases reported elsewhere (King 2007, Islam 2019, Liu 2023 Dalangtan, Latorre 2026, Wildbur 2026).
5. **Covariate quality**: ERA5 precipitation reportedly poor over hyperarid Saudi Arabia (Wang 2025, Nelli 2024).
6. **Capacity vs activity / relic DNA**: Buzzard 2022, Bertagni 2021, Horstmann 2024, Li 2026 eLife, Carini 2020, Blazewicz 2013, Rocca 2015; contamination reporting standards for low-biomass samples (Fierer 2025 Nat Microbiol).

# Robert Hoehndorf scientific writing style: critical adaptation

Source consulted:
`/home/leechuck/Documents/papers/hott/WRITINGSTYLE.md`.

This reference preserves the useful house style while resolving its internal
conflicts and avoiding rules that can reduce accuracy or readability.

## Primary criterion: logical flow of the argument

Robert's explicit priority is the logical flow of the argument; shape comes later
and is secondary. Determine what the passage needs to establish, which premises
and observations support it, and how the conclusion follows. Select and order that
content before deciding on paragraph boundaries or polishing sentences.

For each statement, ask what necessary work it does in this argument. An accurate
fact about the same topic may still be a digression. If removing it leaves the
inference intact and loses no necessary qualification or reproducibility detail,
consider deleting or relocating it. Do not invent a motivation or build an extra
transition simply to retain peripheral material. Preserve necessary intermediate
steps; make missing ones explicit only when the evidence supports them.

Use explanation proportionately to the audience and inference. Defining objects
and operations should make the reasoning follow, without turning every local edit
into a tutorial. A concise deletion can improve logical flow more than several
new explanatory sentences. Evaluate the resulting argument before evaluating its
paragraph shape, rhythm, or resemblance to preferred wording.

## Apply directly

- Prefer active voice when it identifies the responsible actor or makes the
  action clearer. Use passive voice when the actor is irrelevant or the object
  of study should remain the grammatical focus.
- Give readers the context needed to interpret a claim before presenting the
  claim. Do not add a dependent clause mechanically.
- Prefer direct, technically precise sentences. Use a longer multi-clause
  sentence only when the clauses form one inference.
- Calibrate certainty: assert established facts and direct observations;
  use `may`, `can`, or `suggests` for limited or uncertain inferences.
- State what data and results show. Never state what they do not show, mean,
  measure, or imply, and never use "X, not Y" or "X rather than Y" to fence
  off an interpretation the reader has not been given. Write the positive
  statement that carries the information, or delete the sentence.
- Use sentence case for titles and headings unless the venue requires another
  convention.
- Never use em dashes. Replace them with commas, parentheses, colons,
  semicolons, or separate sentences according to the logical relation. In
  LaTeX, do not use `---`.
- Do not use `thus`; use `therefore`.
- Prefix semantic version tags with `v` in prose, for example `PhysioMap
  v1.1.1`.
- Use `record` only for an actual row or serialized data object. For scientific
  content, name the object precisely, for example `axiom`, `assertion`,
  `relation`, `variable`, `value`, or `derivative-sign value`.
- Define every symbol before its first use. When a compact list of formal
  constraints would require readers to decode several new objects at once,
  use a table with one concrete example for each constraint.
- Refer to supplementary material by the shortest specific item, such as a
  subsection, figure, equation, or table. Never use an unqualified `the
  Supplement`. Prefix supplementary item numbers with `S`, and order their
  first citations numerically, for example `Suppl. Fig. S1` before `Suppl.
  Fig. S2`.
- Prefer finite verbs over unnecessary gerunds and nominalizations.
- Preserve domain terminology such as ontology, axiom, embedding, semantic
  similarity, annotation, phenotype, and genotype.
- Use plain verbs where they are equally precise: `use` rather than `utilize`,
  `show` rather than `demonstrate`, `apply` rather than `employ`, `allow` rather
  than `enable`, `reduce` rather than `mitigate`, and `rank` rather than
  `prioritize`.
- Remove editorializers (`fortunately`, `interestingly`, `surprisingly`),
  assertions of obviousness (`clearly`, `obviously`), vague intensifiers
  (`very`, `quite`, `really`), and throat-clearing phrases such as `it is
  important to note`, `in order to`, `a number of`, and `due to the fact that`.
- Use transitions only when they state a real relationship: addition,
  contrast, specification, cause, or consequence.
- Support factual claims with appropriate citations or empirical evidence.
  Report data sources, versions, evaluation splits, sampling, metrics, and
  repository locations when relevant to reproduction.
- Do not print cryptographic digests in a human-facing manuscript or PDF.
  Link to a versioned, machine-readable checksum manifest or metadata file
  that identifies the relevant artifact instead.
- Treat figures as designed scientific arguments rather than collections of
  default plots. In a multi-panel figure, align the panels and use a consistent
  visual grammar for typography, line weights, spacing, panel labels, and
  colour. Give each panel a clear evidential role, remove dead space and
  draft-like annotations, and redesign or separate panels that do not form one
  coherent visual narrative.
- Treat a repository cited by a paper as part of the publication. Its default
  branch and landing page should present the current released resource, its
  semantics, supported operations, limitations, installation, reproducible
  examples, release artifacts, and citation. Keep development history in Git
  history or a changelog rather than making it the repository's public
  narrative. Before claiming availability, verify every link without
  authentication and ensure that the tagged release matches the manuscript.

## Paragraph form and connected reasoning

Write manuscript body text as ordinary prose paragraphs. Do not introduce each
paragraph with a bold label followed by text, such as `**Evaluation.** We ...`,
`\textbf{Evaluation.} We ...`, or `\paragraph{Evaluation}`. Removing the bold
alone is insufficient: turn the label into a sentence that advances the argument.
Use genuine section and subsection headings for sustained divisions of the paper.
Do not promote every paragraph label into a subsection. Preserve bold mathematical
notation, and follow a venue's required run-in format or Robert's explicit request.

Build each paragraph around one question, claim, or explanatory step. Its opening
sentence should locate that step in the argument. Develop it with the necessary
premises, mechanism, evidence, or example in an order the reader can follow. Introduce
an object before using it, and make the subject of a pronoun or comparison clear.
Interpret the evidence when an interpretation adds meaning; do not append a
restatement merely to give every paragraph a concluding sentence. Paragraphs can
vary in length and structure with their scientific purpose.

A shared topic is insufficient to connect paragraphs. Identify what the preceding
paragraph establishes and what the next needs to explain or test. Carry the relevant
object, result, or unresolved question into the next paragraph's opening sentence.
If the logical connection is missing, repair the order or add the missing premise.
Words such as "Furthermore" and "Moreover" cannot supply that reasoning. Do not
invent a causal link, motivation, or experiment merely to make a transition smooth.

Use a private one-sentence-per-paragraph outline to check the progression. Planning
labels such as question, method, finding, and meaning stay in that outline; they
must not appear as run-in labels in the manuscript. For a small revision, inspect
the neighbouring paragraphs so that the replacement still fits the passage.

Illustrative example, assuming these methods and results are supported:

Weak: "**Evaluation.** We divided the data by patient. Accuracy was 0.81.
**Robustness.** We tested a second hospital. Accuracy was 0.73."

Connected prose: "We divided the data by patient so that no patient contributed
samples to both training and testing. The model achieved an accuracy of 0.81 on
the held-out patients."

"To assess whether performance extended beyond the first hospital, we evaluated
the model on patients from a second hospital. Accuracy was 0.73 in this cohort."

The purpose and evaluation design must come from the study evidence; this example
is a pattern for relating supported facts, not permission to supply missing facts.

## Corrections belong in the corrected account

Treat Robert's correction as an instruction to revise the account. Write the
scientific statement that remains true after the correction. Update dependent
sentences, definitions, captions, and cross-references within the requested scope;
flag affected material outside that scope separately. Keep editorial history in
an author-facing change note, a response to reviewers, or version control when
appropriate. The paper's reader has not participated in the editing conversation.

Do not insert an old error, abandoned formulation, failed draft, or explanation of
why wording changed into the manuscript. This includes sentences such as "Previous
versions conflated A with B, whereas the current version separates them", "We now
correctly account for X", and "Following feedback, we clarified Y" when their only
basis is an editing exchange. Do not keep the same comparison after deleting just
"previously" or "now"; replace the whole claim with the supported scientific account.

Illustrative correction: Robert says, "The split was by patient, not by image."

Manuscript: "We partitioned the dataset by patient, with all images from each
patient assigned to the same partition."

Optional author-facing note: "Corrected the split description to patient-level."

The added detail about image assignment is appropriate only if the correction or
source material establishes it. Otherwise state only "We partitioned the dataset
by patient" and resolve any material uncertainty separately.

Retain real scientific history when it is part of the argument: cited prior work,
a documented comparison of released versions, the sequence of a reported experiment,
a correction notice, or a requested reviewer response. Distinguish those evidence-
based comparisons from the history of editing this manuscript. A factual correction
may expose a material methodological flaw; report it to Robert and describe its
scientific consequences accurately rather than concealing it as a prose change.

## Argument patterns

- **Introduction:** problem from general to specific → state-of-the-art
  approaches and successive limitations → solution mapped to those limitations
  → evaluation and impact → availability.
- **Methods:** purpose and output → fully specified inputs → reproducible
  protocol, tools, versions, parameters, and decision rules → provenance,
  quality control, evaluation design, and analyses.
- **Results:** hypothesis or question → brief test → finding with a figure or
  table pointer → interpretation → next hypothesis.
- **Discussion or conclusion:** contribution → relation to prior work →
  limitations → specific future tests.

The Results pattern above supersedes the source guide's weaker instruction to
restate a method and then list metrics.

## Preserve Robert's explanatory style

The revision-based profile below is supported by six inspected pre-2022 edits
in four coauthored manuscripts. It describes preferred explanatory moves, not a
fixed paragraph template or a reason to imitate dated vocabulary. Detailed before/
after evidence is recorded in this skill's `references/revision-evidence.md` and
`references/revision-evidence.json`.

- Preserve the premises of an inference even when shortening. Compress repeated
  setup or peripheral detail before removing the definition, reason, or intermediate
  step that makes the conclusion follow. Retain an already coherent explanation.
- Organize methods and related work around the objects represented, operations
  performed, and properties preserved. Introduce named methods as instances of
  that explanation; avoid a catalogue of names and features.
- Make the reason for each operation recoverable: identify its input, what it
  changes or preserves, and why that matters for the next step. Define a technical
  operation before claiming its benefit. Explain the same scientific object as
  it passes through different representations.
- Introduce technical operations in plain terms at first use, for example
  prediction as computing the consequences of an intervention and abduction as
  identifying an intervention that can explain observations.
- Narrate a contribution through concrete author actions in their scientific
  order: `we collected`, `we classified`, `we encoded`, `we projected`, and
  `we evaluated`. Do not replace this sequence with a dense inventory of
  artifact properties.
- Build conceptual distinctions one at a time and preserve their dependency
  order. State what an object is, why the distinction matters, and only then
  introduce the notation or operation that depends on it.
- In related work, first state what an approach represents or computes, then
  state the limitation that matters for the paper. Avoid compressed contrasts
  that require the reader to reconstruct the missing premise.
- Repeat the precise technical noun when a pronoun or stylistic synonym could
  blur its referent. In particular, keep ontology semantics, causal semantics,
  representation, trait, and intervention distinct.
- Separate the content and semantics of a scientific resource from the
  evidence and provenance used to construct it. Describe admission evidence
  and provenance in Methods; do not present evidence classes as content types
  or as inputs to an application that does not read them. Organize application
  Results around the represented content that actually changes inference.
- When a formalism has several semantic or computational layers, introduce
  them in dependency order. First state what the primary variables denote and
  what the relations constrain. Then define any derivative, sign, symbolic, or
  other abstraction of that semantics. Finally state exactly which abstraction
  an implemented solver reads. Do not let an operational graph or calculus
  stand in for the underlying formalism.
- Keep explanatory prose at the level of scientific objects, formal
  parameters, and method concepts. Do not expose internal source-code
  identifiers, function arguments, schema field paths, enum labels, or fixture
  names as if they were scientific terminology. Use human-readable names
  instead. Retain public artifact names, stable content identifiers, external
  accessions, and exact commands only where they are needed for audit or
  computational reproduction.
- Use examples to perform explanatory work. Show how the defined operation acts
  on a concrete instance, then use that instance to explain its consequence.
  Carry a useful example across adjacent paragraphs rather than replacing it at
  every step. Supply only examples and mechanisms supported by the sources.
- Give each evaluation a question and state what the result establishes. Explain
  why a constraint changes the design or the metric. A table reference alone is
  not a finding. When one result raises a further question, make that question
  explicit before introducing the next comparison; do not invent experimental
  motivation or causation to complete this pattern.
- Use simple signposting such as `First`, `We then`, `Additionally`, `Among
  these`, and `However` when it exposes the actual argumentative sequence.
  Avoid varying transitions merely for style.
- Treat an application such as rare disease as an application of the general
  representation unless the evidence justifies making the application itself
  the paper's identity.
- Preserve this explanatory pacing while correcting draft-level grammar,
  repetition that adds no conceptual clarity, placeholders, and uncalibrated
  words such as `trivially`.

## Apply with judgment

- Do not target an average sentence length of 25–35 words. Vary length according
  to the inference and split sentences when this improves comprehension.
- Do not meet a numerical citation quota. Cite claims at the density required
  by the field and avoid citation padding.
- Do not add decimal places unless the measurement, sample size, and source
  justify that precision.
- Vocabulary preferences are defaults, not substitutions. For example,
  `workflow`, `pipeline`, `limit`, `hinder`, `allow`, and `enable` have
  different meanings. Choose the accurate word.
- Avoid promotional uses of `comprehensive`, `novel`, `unique`, `rigorous`, and
  `robust`, but retain them when they denote a defined property or metric, such
  as a unique top-ranked candidate.
- Do not use stock novelty formulas such as “to the best of our knowledge” or
  “the first” unless a current literature search supports the claim.
- Never leave invented numerical or citation placeholders in a final
  manuscript. Placeholders are acceptable only in an explicitly marked draft.
- Do not explain every correction unless the user requests a teaching edit.
  Report material structural and scientific changes instead.
- Technical precision and readability are joint requirements. When they appear
  to conflict, revise the structure or add necessary context rather than
  sacrificing either one by rule.

# P3 "Life on air": analyses for the ISME Journal paper (9-10 October 2026)

Working record for the split-off atmospheric trace-gas paper (`plans/manuscript-split-2026-10.md`).
Nothing here is yet a registered manuscript claim in `reproducibility/claims.tsv`. Every number below is a
genomic **capacity** measure, not activity or flux. Genome equivalents (GE) are copies per
single-copy-gene genome equivalent. They are relative capacity, not cell fractions: group 1l genomes
carry about 1.9 copies on average and group 1h genomes about 1.1.

## Hypotheses (decided 10 October 2026)

The original P3 hypothesis was a stable, compartment-independent baseline despite strong temporal
turnover. It is contradicted by the data:
- Trip explains about 2% of community composition (`stability/`).
- Compartment has a strong effect on hydB.

The replacement hypotheses were set after an independent second assessment of the same data and literature:

- **H1.** Root-adjacent soil selectively loses group 1l H2 scavengers. Root-adjacent and bulk "Deep" samples
  are both taken at 5-10 cm (data-paper methods), so this is a same-depth contrast. The 1l loss must exceed
  the loss of control markers (other [NiFe] large subunits, non-form-I CoxL).
- **H2.** H2 and CO scavenging capacity are decoupled across microhabitats: high-affinity hydB varies,
  form-I CoxL does not.
- **H3.** Long-term aridity sets the spatial 1l baseline, but between-trip change at a site does not track
  antecedent precipitation (space-for-time substitution fails).

Dropped:
- Functional redundancy with compensatory dynamics. Carrier genera fluctuate in step (phi 0.115 vs null 0.048).
- A persistence advantage of carriers. It is not present within genus or family.
- Rain-pulse responses.
- The RQ/D3 16S capacity index as primary evidence.

## Results (all 573 paired-read samples screened; 550 with metadata and >= 10 SCG genes)

**H1** (`h123/h1_*`; 107 site x trip pairs at 48 sites):
- Group 1l GE is a median log2 −2.23 (about 4.7-fold) lower at roots than in bulk sand at the same depth
  (site bootstrap 95% CI −2.82 to −1.57; lower in 89% of pairs; site-level Wilcoxon p 8e-9).
- Excess over the control markers: −1.97 (CI −2.53 to −1.05, p 3e-8).
- Group 1h shows no excess (−0.06, p 0.90). Form-I CoxL is slightly less depleted than the controls (+0.17).
- Marker x compartment interaction: LRT chi2 308, df 4, p 2e-65. Only the 1l term departs from the reference (−1.74, SE 0.12).
- Holds in all 5 trips (−1.6 to −3.4).
- All ten major 1l-carrier orders are depleted (Acidimicrobiales −2.8, Solirubrobacterales −1.7, Rubrobacterales −2.3, Gaiellales −2.4).

**H2** (`h123/h2_*`):
- Raw-read ratio of high-affinity hydB to form-I coxL reads (no shared normaliser):
  - roots vs bulk: log2 −1.60 (CI −1.94 to −1.15)
  - surface vs bulk: log2 −0.95 (CI −1.45 to −0.48)
- Surface vs bulk: 1l −0.97, form-I CoxL −0.02 (p 0.18).
- Compartment variance share: 1l 20.5%, 1h 5.5%, form-I CoxL 3.1%, controls 6–7%.
- CO capacity is spread over about twice as many effective genera as H2 capacity (about 20 vs 10–11 per sample).

**H3** (`h123/h3_*`; site medians, bulk 5-10 cm, 51 sites):

Spatial:
- 1l vs ERA5 1995–2024 mean annual precipitation: Spearman rho −0.51 (p 1e-4).
- 1l vs NASA POWER 2001–2020: rho −0.43 (p 0.0015).
- Spatial-block bootstrap CI −0.78 to −0.18. Leave-one-block-out range −0.61 to −0.37.
- Partial on latitude/longitude/elevation: −0.48 (p 4e-4).
- Mixed model across compartments: 1l −0.49 log2 per SD log MAP (p 4e-5); 1h −0.14 (p 0.09); form-I CoxL −0.04 (p 0.41).
- **Caveat:** after partialling out the first community-composition axis, rho falls to −0.20 (p 0.16).
  Aridity and the main composition axis covary, and 1l carriers are a large part of that axis.

Temporal:
- Within-site, between-trip change does not track antecedent precipitation in bulk soil (P365 rho 0.02).
- The 95% CI of the temporal slope (−1.57 to 1.70 per log unit) excludes the spatial slope (−4.15).
- Surface 1l rises after wet years (rho 0.38), but this is aliased with trip: trip-adjusted p 0.31.
  Form-I CoxL keeps a trip-adjusted surface response (p 0.010).

**Sensitivity** (`h123/sensitivity.tsv`): the H1 and H3 effects are stable at identity 50/60/70%, with
contamination-flagged reps removed, without trip 5 (excess −1.61), and without trip 4 (101-nt libraries;
excess −1.74).

**Weak 1l calls** (Alejandra's open item B9: weakest 1l call 37.8% identity / 20.5% coverage):
- Restricting the 1l label to the 1,002 of 1,124 EQ 1l proteins typed at >= 50% identity and >= 80% coverage
  gives an H1 log2 fold change of −2.19 (all calls: −2.23). Weak calls contribute 0.07 GE in Deep.
- Registered as sensitivity variant `strict_1l_calls` (reads to the 122 weak proteins in `reads/weak_1l_proteins.txt`
  discarded, `reads/guild_genome_equivalents_strict1l.tsv`): ratio −2.19, excess −1.97.

**Operon context** (`operon/`; loci with >= 5 genes on both sides):
- 1h: small subunit adjacent 84%, maturation genes 96%.
- 1l: 73% and 88%. Recurrent DUF948 and NifU neighbours.
- Form-I CoxL: 73% in complete coxMSL operons.

**Contamination** (`decontam/`):
- 376 reps flagged by blank genus. No rep shows the decontam frequency signature (3,453 tested; minimum score 0.19).
- Guild CoverM abundance: 18.2% → 15.9% of the whole community after removing flagged reps.

## H1 mechanism proxies (`h1_mechanism/`, 10 October 2026)

Soil organic carbon was never measured: P1 has XRF elements, pH and weather only, and the RQ26 confounder
audit lists organic carbon as unmeasured. Li et al. 2023's organic-carbon mechanism is therefore tested
through two proxies.

**M1, plant input (NDVI, 60-day window, site x trip).** Mixed models with log MAP and site/trip random effects:
- 1l falls with NDVI in bulk 5-10 cm (−0.64 log2 per SD, SE 0.16) and at the surface (−1.23, SE 0.15).
  This is independent of MAP; site NDVI vs MAP rho is 0.57.
- Root-adjacent 1l is flat (−0.02).
- The root excess loss is smaller at greener sites (site-median rho +0.36, p 0.014).
- Reading: vegetation depresses 1l throughout the soil, and the root contrast shrinks where bulk soil is
  already plant-influenced.

**M2, copiotrophy (gRodon2 2.7.3 predicted minimum doubling time).** Job 53541094; 4,817 of 5,438 reps
estimated. The first run, 53541016, matched only old ribosomal-protein names and is superseded.
- **a. Carriers grow slowly.** Within order: 1l +0.12 log doubling time (SE 0.03; medians 4.2 vs 3.3 h);
  1h +0.11; form-I CoxL +0.06. Oligotrophy is shared by 1h carriers, so it cannot by itself explain why only
  1l is lost.
- **b. Root-adjacent communities are more copiotrophic.** The abundance-weighted log doubling time is
  −0.25 lower than in bulk soil (faster in 79% of 106 pairs; site Wilcoxon p 1.5e-5). The surface shift is
  −0.29.
- **c. The 1l loss scales with that shift, but only partly.**
  - Excess loss vs shift: Spearman rho 0.22 (p 0.023); lmer slope 1.55 log2 per log unit (SE 0.62).
  - The predicted excess loss at zero shift is still −1.59. Copiotrophy accounts for a minor part of
    the −1.97 excess.
- **d. Carrying 1l predicts root depletion beyond growth rate and phylum.** 288 genera with ≥0.05% mean
  bulk abundance; genus root log2 fold change regressed on median log doubling time and the carrier
  fractions:
  - growth slope −0.37 (p 6e-4): slow growers are depleted;
  - fraction carrying 1l −0.75 (p 3e-5);
  - 1h (+0.07) and form-I (−0.02) carriage are not associated with depletion;
  - R² 0.30.

**Interpretation for H1.** Two findings are consistent with Li et al.'s plant-carbon/copiotroph mechanism:
root-adjacent soil shifts to faster growers, and greener sites have less 1l in bulk soil. But a large
1l-specific loss remains after growth rate, phylum and the community shift are accounted for. 1h carriers
are equally slow-growing and are not lost. H1 therefore stands as a group-1l-specific effect. Organic carbon
availability is a likely contributor, not the whole explanation. A remaining candidate is the higher
H2/O2 or moisture regime beside roots, which would favour 1h over 1l; this is untested.

## Literature read on 10 October 2026 (`../literature/`)

**Li et al. 2023, ISME J (fertile islands)** is the closest precedent for H1.
- Mu Us Desert, semiarid (180 mm). 60 metagenomes under and outside Hedysarum shrubs, 0-60 cm.
- H2- and CO-oxidation genes are lower under shrubs, most strongly at 0-10 cm. H2 genes track soil and
  dissolved organic carbon (rho −0.48, −0.43); CO genes track dissolved organic carbon only (−0.31).
- Added soluble carbon suppresses measured H2 and CO uptake. The trace-gas oxidisers are oligotrophs.
- Consequence: "plants deplete trace-gas oxidisers" is not new and H1 must cite it. What H1 adds:
  - specific to group 1l, with 1h unchanged (Li did not separate 1h from 1l; their typing names group 1d/2a);
  - a loss beyond control genes, with copies per genome rather than TPM;
  - root-adjacent soil against bulk soil at the same depth;
  - hyperarid sand, 48 sites and 5 trips;
  - form-I CO capacity not depleted. Li found a weaker CO decline, so this extends H2 rather than contradicting it.
- Li's organic-carbon mechanism is the obvious explanation for H1. Soil organic carbon was never measured;
  see the proxy tests under "H1 mechanism proxies" above.

**Tribbia et al. 2026, Appl Environ Microbiol (Namib fog/aridity transect)** supports the direction of H3.
- 10 sites, 1-5 cm, one time point, activity measurements and amplicons, no gene typing.
- Biomass, ATP and respiration fall with aridity, but atmospheric H2 oxidation per 16S copy is highest in
  the most arid fog zone. Rubrobacteriaceae are enriched there.
- It neither tests nor contradicts the H3 temporal result.
- Practical point for a gas-uptake add-on: their assays used 2 g of soil stored at 4 °C, not frozen.

Neither paper bears on P4 (the dark proteome) or P2 (ecology).

## Open items

- Manuscript draft exists (`../manuscript/main.tex`); register its headline numbers in `reproducibility/verify.py` and `claims.tsv`.
- H3 composition-axis confound: decide whether a causal-mediation framing (aridity → lineages → 1l) is defensible.
- Relic DNA: only 9 PMA pairs exist; state as a limitation.
- Optional: gas-chromatography uptake on archived soil (2–3 g at −20 °C is workable for potential rates).

# Round 1 literature digest (Gemini 3.8 Flash via agy, 8 search directions, 12 Oct 2026)

Candidates were proposed by Flash and every DOI was checked against Crossref (title match); 5 mismatched DOIs were corrected by hand and none of the retained DOIs is fabricated. **The one-line relevance notes are Flash summaries and are NOT verified**: check the local PDF/XML before relying on any claim. Local files are in `lifeonair/literature/<key>.pdf` or `<key>.fulltext.xml`; "no local copy" means paywalled (citation + DOI only).

Search prompts: `search/round1/prompts/`; raw outputs with Flash syntheses: `search/round1/out/`.

| jespersen_2025_diverse-lineages-adaptations-oxygen | 10.1016/j.tibs.2025.04.006 | context | Delineates the evolutionary lineages, gas channel architectures, and FeS cluster adaptations that differentiate group 1h, 1l, and 2a oxygen-insensitive [NiFe]-hydrogenases. | no local copy |
| greening_2022_hydrogen-major-lifeline-aerobic | 10.1016/j.tim.2021.08.004 | context | Synthesizes ecological principles of atmospheric H2 consumption across Actinobacteriota and Chloroflexota, contrasting persistence-driven starvation survival with mixotrophic growth. | no local copy |
| islam_2023_microbial-hydrogen-cycling-agricultural | 10.1111/1751-7915.14300 | context | Synthesizes plant rhizosphere interactions with hydrogen-oxidising bacteria, examining how root environments, diazotrophs, and soil gas sinks interlock. | XML |
| berdugo_2020_global-ecosystem-thresholds-driven | 10.1126/science.aay5958 | context | Defines the non-linear aridity threshold beyond which vascular plant cover collapses globally, establishing the environmental regime where soil microbes must transition to atmospheric trace gases for energy. | no local copy |
| bulgarelli_2013_structure-functions-bacterial-microbiota | 10.1146/annurev-arplant-050312-120106 | context | Details the two-step selection model where root exudates systematically enrich fast-growing copiotrophs and deplete oligotrophic taxa, supporting a compositional artefact explanation for group 1l reduction. | no local copy |
| garciapichel_2023_microbiology-biological-crusts | 10.1146/annurev-micro-032521-015202 | context | Synthesizes biocrust metabolic organization, providing a benchmark for phototrophic, surface-bound primary productivity that contrasts sharply with the chemosynthetic bulk sands of hyperarid dune systems. | no local copy |

| warwick_2023_atmospheric-composition-climate-impacts | 10.5194/acp-23-13451-2023 | context | Demonstrates in Earth system models that soil deposition is the dominant removal pathway mitigating atmospheric H2 accumulation and associated indirect greenhouse forcing. | PDF |
| paulot_2024_reanalysis-noaa-observations-implications | 10.5194/acp-24-4217-2024 | context | Constrains the global tropospheric H2 budget and deposition velocity using atmospheric monitoring, establishing the macroscopic scale of the dryland soil sink. | PDF |

## H1 (19)

| key | DOI | stance | relevance (Flash, unverified) | local |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| delaporte_2024_rhizosphere-effect-promotes-persistence | 10.1016/j.soilbio.2024.109599 | challenges | Demonstrates that rhizosphere carbon inputs can promote rather than suppress the persistence of trace gas oxidizing activity in nutrient-poor soils, challenging the universality of root-adjacent capacity depletion. | no local copy |
| lopez_2023_growth-rate-dominant-factor | 10.1038/s41396-023-01453-6 | challenges | Finds that predicted maximal growth rate is the primary driver of rhizosphere enrichment across global soil MAGs, directly challenging our finding that growth rate explains only a minor part of root-adjacent shifts. | PDF |
| kropp_2026_atmospheric-hydrogen-consumption-regulated | 10.1128/msystems.01678-25 | challenges | Demonstrates that specific organic carbon metabolites repress high-affinity hydrogenase expression via GylR, challenging the assumption that broad plant-carbon proxies should linearly predict root-adjacent capacity losses. | XML |
| azarbad_2022_relative-quantitative-rhizosphere-microbiome | 10.3389/fmicb.2021.798023 | challenges | Shows that per-cell relative profiling produces spurious apparent depletion of soil oligotrophs in the rhizosphere due to copiotroph biomass expansion, warning that 1l loss could be a compositional artifact. | PDF |
| dip_2025_potential-biofertilization-effect-accompanied | 10.7717/peerj.20019 | challenges | Observes that plant roots and elevated H2 exert surprisingly modest restructuring on overall rhizosphere community composition, challenging the notion that plant roots dramatically repress all hydrogenotrophs universally. | PDF |
| zhang_2026_divergent-organic-carbon-accrual | 10.1016/j.agee.2025.110075 | context | Shows that plant root inputs in hyperarid soils create localized carbon accrual zones that diverge strongly from surrounding desert sands, setting the edaphic contrast between root-adjacent and bulk soils. | no local copy |
| delaporte_2020_gaseous-milieu-extending-boundaries | 10.1016/j.tim.2020.02.016 | context | Establishes a mechanistic framework for how root exudation and gas diffusion govern trace gas (H2 and CO) concentrations and microbial scavenging dynamics in the rhizosphere. | no local copy |
| mapelli_2018_stage-development-modulates-rhizosphere | 10.1038/s41396-017-0026-4 | context | Shows that roots exert a potent selective rhizosphere effect that filters soil microbial assemblages even in extreme barren deserts, directly bearing on the marked depletion of group 1l scavengers in root-adjacent soils. | PDF |
| chiri_2021_termite-emissions-select-hydrogenotrophic | 10.1073/pnas.2102625118 | context | Shows that localized gas emission hotspots shift microbial communities away from high-affinity atmospheric scavengers toward lower-affinity enzymes, providing an analogue for root-adjacent microenvironments. | no local copy |
| he_2025_microbial-life-history-strategies | 10.1128/msystems.00178-25 | context | Demonstrates that carbon-poor soils favor oligotrophic genomic traits that are rapidly selected against upon organic carbon input, illuminating why root-adjacent soils lose specialized group 1l scavengers. | XML |

## Cross-direction themes raised by Flash (to be checked, not established)

1. **Compositional artefact for H1**: relative (per-genome) profiling can show oligotroph "loss" when rhizosphere biomass expands (Azarbad 2022); growth rate is reported as a dominant predictor of the rhizosphere effect (Lopez et al. 2023 ISME J), which bears on the claim that growth rate explains only a minor part of the 1l loss.
2. **Group 1l physiology**: Imminger 2024 (Nat Commun) reportedly finds desert 1l expression responsive to hydration; Kropp 2026 (mSystems) catabolite repression of high-affinity hydrogenase by glycerol; Baril & Constant 2023 labile-carbon repression; de la Porte 2024 rhizosphere effect promoting gas-oxidation persistence (contrary direction).
3. **Space-for-time**: failure of substitution is predicted by theory when temporal variance is small relative to the spatial gradient (Blois 2013, Damgaard 2019, Perret 2024, Lovell 2023, Knapp 2026 aridity vs drought); legacy effects (Hawkes 2017, Canarini 2021). Risk that "space-for-time fails" is framed as more novel/strong than a 31-month design permits.
4. **H2 novelty**: broader taxonomic spread/redundancy of form-I CoxL than of high-affinity hydrogenases reported elsewhere (King 2007, Islam 2019, Liu 2023 Dalangtan, Latorre 2026, Wildbur 2026).
5. **Covariate quality**: ERA5 precipitation reportedly poor over hyperarid Saudi Arabia (Wang 2025, Nelli 2024).
6. **Capacity vs activity / relic DNA**: Buzzard 2022, Bertagni 2021, Horstmann 2024, Li 2026 eLife, Carini 2020, Blazewicz 2013, Rocca 2015; contamination reporting standards for low-biomass samples (Fierer 2025 Nat Microbiol).
